We need to make the test pass or at least not fail.\n\nLet's look at the test again in `test_export.py`:\n\n```python\ndef test_render_marks_last_row_as_total():\n    assert render_rows([[1, 2], [3, 4]], mark_last=True) == [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]\n```\n\nThis test expects that when `render_rows` is called with `mark_last=True`, it returns the rows with the last row marked as total by prefixing \"TOTAL\" to the last row's values? Actually, the last row is [3,4], and the expected output is `[\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]`. So the first row is rendered normally, the second row is rendered as \"TOTAL,3,4\". That suggests that `render_rows` should detect the `mark_last` flag and modify the last row accordingly.\n\nBut our `export_csv` function no longer relies on `render_rows` to mark the last row; it appends a separate total row. However, the test `test_render_marks_last_row_as_total` is still present and expects `render_rows` to have this behavior. We have two possibilities:\n\n- Change `render_rows` to support `mark_last` parameter and implement it. Then the test will pass.\n- Change the test to not use `mark_last`, or remove the test.\n\nGiven that we already changed `export_csv` to use `column_totals` and append a total row, maybe the `mark_last` functionality is no longer needed. But the test is there, so we should fix it to pass with the current `render_rows` or update `render_rows`.\n\nLet's see if we can implement `mark_last` in `render_rows`.
